いが、好調なAI関連輸出が景気を下支え。

資ともに押し上げ。日銀は物価上振れリ
スクを意識し利上げを前倒し
内需が力強さを欠くなか、強固な製造能
力を背景とした輸出攻勢が経済を支える

ん引。消費も減税や資産効果を受け底堅

力後退を確認し27年末頃より利下げ転換

投資等が下支え。先行きもAI・レジリエン

原油だけでなく石油精製品や天然ガスなどにも供給制約。インフレ圧力は長期化
◼ 原油高に加え、製油能力逼迫に伴うクラック・スプレッドの上昇もグローバルなインフレを助長する懸念
― クラック・スプレッド（石油精製品と原油の価格差）は例年を大きく上回る水準。戦争の影響を背景に中東・ロシアからの
輸出が減少していること等が影響
◼ 代替ルートの乏しい天然ガスや肥料（尿素など）の供給懸念は継続。先物価格は高止まり
― 天然ガスでは中東依存度が高い欧州やアジアで価格が上昇。欧州ガス価格（TTF）は前年比で約2.4倍まで急騰

― 先物価格も需給ひっ迫継続を見越して高止まり。中東情勢次第では、天然ガス価格がさらに上振れる可能性も

強まる投資主導型経済の様相。拡大するAI・レジリエンス投資が世界経済の成長をけん引

（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

◼ 原油供給回復の遅れ
◼ 石油精製能力の逼迫

◼ 設備・資材需要の増加
◼ 電力・人材需要の増加
◼ 資金需要の拡大

インフレ長期化：エネルギー供給ショックや投資需要がインフレ圧力に
◼ ホルムズ海峡の封鎖長期化により、エネルギー価格は当面高止まり
― 開戦以降、グローバルCPIのエネルギー指数は前年比約15％まで上昇。原油や石化製品などの高騰が、航空運賃など
のサービス価格や、輸送コストの上昇を通じた幅広い財価格に波及
◼ 旺盛なAI関連需要が物価を押し上げ
― データセンター投資需要を受けメモリー価格が高騰。PC・スマホ等の最終製品価格へも波及
― 需要拡大に伴う電力価格の上昇も相まって、供給ショックと投資需要が同時に物価を押し上げ

（注） 政府機関閉鎖による米CPIデータ欠損のため、2025年10月は欠値

高金利の常態化：政策金利に加え、タームプレミアムも金利を押し上げ
◼ 主要国・地域の中銀はインフレ警戒から利上げモードへ移行
― ホルムズ危機長期化や投資需要の強さに伴うインフレ圧力の高まりを受け、日米欧の中銀はいずれも9月に利上げ
◼ 日本以外の主要国においても、政策金利の上昇織り込みや財政懸念を背景に長期金利は上昇
― 米国では財政懸念に加え、足元では政策金利の上昇織り込みが長期金利を押し上げ

### Page 13

成長・負担の偏在が広げる「耐性格差」―4つの緊張関係
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
②企業・家計間の緊張
生産性上昇
企業収益増加
遅れる賃金
・所得への波及
③企業間の緊張
大企業
／AI・戦略分野
中小企業
／非AI・戦略分野
④家計間の緊張
資産保有層
／高所得層
資産非保有層
／中低所得層
①国家間の緊張
技術／資本／資源
を持つ国
技術／資本／資源
を持たない国
→
成長の実感乏しく、社会的受容性が低下
→
保護主義・産業政策競争が加速
→
消費・資産形成力の差が累積
→
投資余力の差が次の成長機会を左右
格差は一時的か、固定化・自己増幅するか
（耐性格差が強まる中での日本の立ち位置は？）
投資主導経済＋インフレ＋高金利 拡大する耐性格差成長・負担の偏在
偏在化する成長の果実「新常態」下の金融市場中東危機長期化、加速するAI・レジリエンス投資

### Page 14

「耐性格差」は固定化するのか―成長の持続性を見極める3つの評価軸
（出所） みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
見極める軸 ポイント 代表指標 含意
波及 成長の果実は広がるか
◼ 生産性
◼ 実質賃金
◼ AI導入率
◼ 中小企業収益
波及すれば成長の裾野が拡大
しなければ成長の偏りが固定化
機会 「持たざる側」も次の成長機会に参加できるか
◼ 労働移動
◼ 新規参入
◼ 資金調達格差
◼ 人的資本投資
参加できれば格差は流動的
できなければ勝者・敗者が固定化
底上げ 格差が残る中でも、弱い主体の生活水準は改善するか
◼ ジニ係数（再分配）
◼ リスキリング
◼ 資産形成
◼ 社会移動
改善すれば社会的受容性を維持
しなければ分断圧力が強まる
問われるのは格差そのものではなく、偏在が成長の持続性を損なうか
⇒成長の果実が広がり、次の機会と生活水準の改善につながるかが焦点
評
価
軸
と
そ
の
含
意
問
題
設
定
「波及・機会・底上げ」を通じて、成長の持続性をモニタリング
偏在化する成長の果実「新常態」下の金融市場中東危機長期化、加速するAI・レジリエンス投資

14

### Page 15

参考①（国家間）：競争力の基盤は国ごとに偏在、資源高は日本に逆風
◼ 国家の競争力は、資源・製造能力・技術力・資金動員力・市場規模など複数の基盤に左右
― 日本は製造能力・技術力に強みを持つ一方、資源面の脆弱性から資源高局面では交易条件が悪化。また、潤沢な資金
（金融資産）を有しているものの、国内成長分野への資金動員力に課題
― 台湾・韓国では資源高で輸入価格が上昇する一方、半導体などAI関連財の価格上昇が輸出価格を押し上げ、交易条件
を下支え
国家の競争力を支える5つの基盤
競争力を支える基盤 強みを有する国・地域
①資源
エネルギー・重要鉱物などを安定的に確保できる力

中国、豪州
②製造能力

米国、中国、

大きな内需を背景に新技術を普及・量産できる力

参考②（企業・家計間）：生産性向上に遅れる賃金、低下する労働分配率
◼ 日本では時間当たり生産性が上昇する一方、1人当たり実質賃金の伸びは限定的
― 働き方改革など労働時間の短縮も両者の乖離に影響。交易条件悪化による所得流出も実質賃金を下押しする一因に
― 対して、AIブームの恩恵が大きい米国や韓国では、労働生産性と実質賃金がともに上昇する好循環

◼ 日本の労働分配率は主要国・地域と比較しても低下傾向。企業収益の賃金への還元が今後の焦点
― 米国や韓国においても足元で低下がみられるが、AI需要の拡大による企業収益の伸びが主因
― 日本では雇用者報酬の伸びが緩やかであり、生産性向上の成果を賃金に繋げられるかどうかが課題
（注） 労働生産性は総労働時間あたりの実質GDP、実質賃金は雇用者1人あたり
（出所） OECDより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
労働生産性と実質賃金 主要国・地域における労働分配率の推移
（注） 自営業者の労働所得を調整した雇用者報酬の名目GDP比。韓国の2024年以降は
OECDのデータを用いて延長
（出所） 欧州委員会、OECDより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

### Page 17

参考③（企業間）：金利上昇への耐性は企業規模・業種で大きな格差
◼ 日本企業の投下資本利益率（ROIC）は規模・業種間で大きな格差
― 総じてみれば、企業規模が小さいほどROICが低い傾向（中堅企業は例外）。特に小規模企業は1.5％と低水準
― 業種別では、製造業の輸送用機械や、非製造業の不動産、電力・ガス、運輸・郵便、医療・福祉などでROICが低い傾向。
なお、情報通信や宿泊・飲食は大企業のROICが相対的に高い一方、小規模企業が低く、業種内の格差が大きい
◼ インタレストカバレッジレシオ（ICR）も規模・業種間で格差。ROIC・ICRともに低い規模・業種ほど金利上昇に脆弱な構造
― ICRも規模が小さいほど低位（中堅企業は例外）。業種別では、不動産、電力・ガス、運輸・郵便、医療・福祉等が低水準
（注） 投下資本利益率（ROIC）＝税引き後営業利益÷（有利子負債＋自己資本）。大企業
は資本金10億円以上、中堅企業は1億円以上10億円未満、中小企業は1千万円以上
1億円未満、小規模企業は1千万円未満

（出所） 財務省「法人企業統計調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

（注） インタレストカバレッジレシオ（ICR）＝営業利益÷支払利息。大企業は資本金10億円

低所得世帯 高所得世帯
参考④（家計間）：金融資産を保有する層と持たざる層の間でインフレ耐性に格差
◼ 所得階級によって資産形成余力に格差。低所得世帯ほど資産形成が進みにくく、インフレ耐性が脆弱
― 高所得世帯では貯蓄余力が大きい一方、低所得世帯では物価高による生活費の増加が重石となり、貯蓄率は低水準
― 低所得世帯では金融資産の蓄積が進みにくく、インフレによる実質所得の目減りを資産所得で補う余地も限定的
◼ 資産形成余力に加え、資産選択も二極化。インフレ下では資産格差が拡大し、インフレ耐性の差も広がりやすい構造
― 余裕資金のある高所得世帯は収益性重視のリスク資産投資に積極的。一方、中低所得世帯はリスク資産投資に消極的
― インフレは資産形成余力と資産選択の違いを増幅させ、資産格差とインフレ耐性の格差がさらに拡大する可能性
（注） 勤労者世帯の平均貯蓄率。平均貯蓄率＝貯蓄純増 ÷ 可処分所得 × 100。対象

世帯・項目の違いにより、GDPベースの貯蓄率とは一致しない
（出所） 総務省「家計調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

（注） 対象は金融資産を保有する二人以上世帯
（出所） J-FLEC「家計の金融行動に関する世論調査」より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

（１）米国：AIによる景気押し上げは当面継続。物価高から FRBは一時的に政策金利を引き上げへ
◼ 2026・27年の実質GDP成長率はいずれも前年比＋2.2％と予想
― 設備投資は、2027年入り後に巡航速度並みの伸び率へ回帰。ハイパースケーラーは投資効率の低下を意識し、AIの普
及ペースを見極めながら投資を継続
― 個人消費は減税政策や資産効果により底堅く推移。2027年入り後はインフレ減速に伴う実質所得の改善も支えに
◼ FRBは関税引き上げや原油高によるインフレ上振れリスクを警戒し、2026年内に追加で1回の利上げ。関税・原油高の影
響が一巡した後、 物価の十分な減速が確認される2027年後半以降に2回の利下げを予想

◼ 2026年後半は、熱波を受けた生産抑制やインフレによる消費下押しなど逆風はありつつも、財政拡張が下支え

### Page 32

欧州：熱波による河川水位低下が景気下押し要因に
◼ 河川の水位低下による生産下振れが7～9月期の成長率を下押し
― 高温と少雨を背景に欧州各地では河川の水位が低下。内陸物流において重要な役割を担うライン川の一部地点では、6
月以降、船舶の積載量に制約が生じる水位が継続
― ライン川の水位がおおよそ100㎝を切ったあたりから10㎝低下するごとにドイツの生産が0.4％程度減少すると試算
― 実際、船舶の輸送機能低下による原材料の供給制約を受けて化学・鉄鋼等の一部素材業種では生産抑制の動き。7～
9月期の経済下押し要因に
ライン川の水位とドイツ鉱工業生産の関係
（注） 閾値モデルを用いてライン川の水位がドイツ鉱工業生産に与える影響を推計。縦軸
の鉱工業生産は景気要因をコントロール（ドイツ鉱工業生産を世界の鉱工業生産に

◼ 天然ガスや農作物価格上昇の影響は今年後半から来年のインフレ押し上げ要因に
― 熱波による発電所の停止や農作物の不作懸念等を背景に、欧州の天然ガス・農産物の先物価格は上昇。これらの価格
上昇がインフレに与える影響は、先行き1年程度残存する計算

― 需給が緩和するにつれ、エネルギー・食料品そのものによる物価押し上げ幅は縮小へ。ただし、インフレ期待の上昇を招

### Page 36

（３）新興国：中国は景気減速が継続。アジアは原油調達の安定や AIブームが追い風に
◼ 中国は景気の減速傾向が続く見通し。2026年の成長率は4.3％、2027年は3.8％まで低下すると予想
― 内需は力強さを欠く。消費は補助金の反動で低迷が継続。投資も過当競争や地方財政難を受けて振るわず
― 外需は景気を下支え。EVなどの製品で輸出攻勢が続き、純輸出がプラスに寄与
◼ アジアでは原油の調達不安が後退、AIブームが景気をけん引。一方、インフレ・金利上昇圧力の高まりには留意が必要
― 原油調達は、代替輸入やバイオ燃料等の活用で懸念が後退。AIブームによる財輸出や域内投資のけん引が継続
― 他方、原油高の再燃やAIブームの継続がインフレ圧力を高める構図。金融政策は引き締め方向へ緩やかにシフト

中国：政府目標は達成圏内だが成長の勢いは弱い。内需が力強さを欠くなか、デフレ圧力は継続
◼ 2026年上期の実質GDP成長率は前年比＋4.7％。政府目標（＋4.5％～5.0％）の範囲内だが、内需の弱さが目立つ結果
― 4～6月期は同＋4.3％と2022年Q4（＋3.0％）以来の低水準。中東情勢を受けた供給ショックもあり、最終消費と総資本
形成が前期から減速。内需の弱さを純輸出（外需）が補う構図は変わらず

中国：原油輸入量は大幅減も、依然供給不足には至らず。代替燃料を用いた生産も拡大
◼ ホルムズ海峡の封鎖により、原油の輸入量は2ケタ減が続く。ただ、需要の低迷もあり供給不足には陥らず
― 海上輸送による原油輸入（3～9月）は封鎖前（10月～2月）対比▲45.0％。ロシアからの代替輸入も限定的
― ガソリンの消費量は3～8月に前年比▲8.8％、ディーゼルは同▲10.7％と低迷しており、需給のひっ迫を回避

中国：消費・投資の低迷が続く一方、輸出と生産は堅調。外需に成長を依存する構図が継続
◼ 消費・投資は減速に歯止めかからず。内需は一段と低迷
― 投資は前年比▲10％超の減少が継続。不動産不況、過当競争、地方財政難によるインフラ投資減速が背景
― 小売も減速傾向が継続。補助金の反動による自動車販売の減少等が下押し
― 一方、生産は引き続き底堅く推移。堅調な輸出を追い風に、コンピューター・通信機器などを中心に高い伸び継続
◼ 輸出はAIブームと自動車の輸出攻勢が継続。低迷する内需を外需の伸びで支える構図
― 輸出拡大は産業政策による高度化の成果と言える一方、貿易不均衡の拡大に対する懸念も（後述）

中国：好調な輸出の一方で輸入は停滞。背景に「自立自強」の進展
◼ 輸入は2010年代中盤以降に頭打ち。「自立自強」による輸入代替の進展が背景にあると考えられる
― かつて加工貿易が主流であった頃は、輸出製品を生産するため中間財や資本財の輸入が増え、先進国にも恩恵
― 世界の輸出入に占める中国のシェアは、輸出がほぼ一貫して上昇している一方、輸入は2010年代中盤以降に頭打ち
― 世界の輸入に占める中国のシェアを品目別に見ても、2024年は多くの業種で2015年からシェアが縮小
― 輸出伸長、輸入停滞の結果、貿易不均衡が拡大。先進各国はこれに懸念表明。貿易摩擦が再び強まるおそれも

アジア：原油高とAIブーム影響で生産コストが膨張。消費者価格への転嫁でインフレ圧力が高まる
◼ 企業の生産コストは増大。原油高を受けてエネルギーや石油由来製品の価格が急騰するほか、AIブーム下のメモリ高騰を
背景に、コンピューターや電気機器類の価格にも上昇圧力
― 主要国の生産者物価は総じて上昇ペースが加速。石油や有機化学製品、コンピューター類の品目が押し上げ
◼ 生産コストは消費者価格に波及。原油高がエネルギー費目を押し上げるほか、スマホやPCの価格も一部でインフレ要因に

― 幅広い地域でエネルギー部門がインフレ率を押し上げ。ガソリンや燃料（LPガス等）のほか、航空運賃の上昇が寄与
― 寄与度は小さいものの、スマホやPCなどの電子機器価格も上昇。メモリ価格の高騰が最終品価格に波及している模様
消費者物価：原油高・AIブーム影響

◼ インフレ圧力と通貨安のもと、金融政策は引き締めにシフト。ただし、内需下押し懸念から政策スタンスは域内でまちまちに

（４）日本：26年度は中東情勢影響のなかでも堅調に推移。 27年度は政策が消費・投資を押し上げ
◼ 2026年度の実質GDP成長率は前年比＋0.8％と予測。中東情勢緊迫化の影響を受ける一方、AI関連需要が下支え
― 中東情勢緊迫化は、原油価格上昇による交易利得・損失の悪化（実質的な所得の海外流出）や先行き不透明感の高ま
りを通じ、消費・投資を抑制。一方、AI・半導体関連輸出の拡大や投資促進税制の開始を受け、堅調な成長に
◼ 2027年度は同＋1.3％と予測。消費税減税の開始による個人消費の押し上げに加え、国内投資拡大がけん引

― 消費税減税が物価上昇の抑制を通じて実質賃金の改善要因に。また、政府の「強く豊かな日本投資枠」による戦略17分
野への支援開始に伴い、設備投資の拡大が成長率を押し上げると予想
（注） 網掛けは予測値

### Page 49

日本：コアCPIは2026年度末にかけて一時前年比＋3％程度に。日銀は2％まで利上げ継続
◼ AIブームの恩恵を受け、企業業績は中東情勢影響のなかでも改善傾向を維持
― 企業の好業績や人手不足の強さを背景に、2027年度の春闘でも5％程度の高い賃上げ率が続くと予想
◼ コアCPI（生鮮食品を除く総合）は、2026年度末にかけて一時的に前年比＋3％程度へ加速
― 原油価格の上昇が消費者物価を押し上げ。物価上振れリスクに対応するため、日銀は2％まで追加利上げを実施へ
― 2027年度に入ると、原油価格が徐々に落ち着くほか、利上げがインフレ率の上昇を抑制。加えて消費税減税が物価を一
段と下押しし、コアCPIが一時的に前年比＋1％台半ばへ低下する見込み
（注） 1. 網掛けは予測値。変化率は、水準のデータからみずほ総合研究所調査部が計算した値であり、公表値と一致しないことがある
2. 経常利益は法人企業統計調査の全規模・全産業（除く金融・保険）ベース
3. 消費者物価の「除く制度要因」は、政府による電気・ガス料金支援、ガソリン補助・暫定税率廃止、高校授業料無償化、消費税減税等の影響を除いて計算

日本：2026年度の成長率を上方修正。AI関連を中心とする輸出の堅調さが主な要因
◼ 2026年度の実質GDP成長率（＋0.8％）は、前回6月見通し（＋0.5％）対比で0.3％Ptの上方修正
― 米国を中心とする旺盛なAI関連需要の恩恵を受け、財輸出が堅調に拡大するとの見方が、26年度上方修正の主因
― 輸入価格上昇を受けた交易利得・損失の悪化（実質的な所得の海外流出）は、個人消費や設備投資などの抑制要因と
して残るものの、輸出の堅調な拡大が景気下押し圧力を一部緩和
◼ 2027年度は、消費税減税の開始や投資促進政策の効果発現により、引き続き高い成長を予想
― ただし、インフレ率の上振れを受けて実質所得の伸びがやや弱まることなどから、前回6月対比で小幅に下方修正

日本：政府が掲げる「2040年度：250兆円」の国内投資目標は、概ね実現可能な範囲
◼ 投資促進税制（26～28年度）に加え、戦略17分野を中心として「強く豊かな日本投資枠」（27～40年度）による支援を実施
― 「強く豊かな日本投資枠」は、27年度概算要求ベース（一般・特別会計）で14.2兆円と大規模
◼ 設備投資は自然体でも2040年度に230兆円へ到達する計算。政府目標は概ね現実的な範囲

日本：輸入増の影響を考慮しても、投資拡大が名目GDPの水準を＋0.5％程度押し上げ
◼ 日本の資本財供給では輸入浸透度が上昇。グローバルな資本財需給ひっ迫は国内供給力強化の足かせに
― とりわけ、電気機械・情報通信機械では輸入依存度の高まりが顕著
― 半導体等を中心にグローバルな需給ひっ迫が続くとみられ、AI・半導体分野等を中心に投資完了には時間
◼ もっとも、輸入増による需要の海外漏出を考慮しても、国内生産増を通じたGDP押し上げ効果は相応の規模
― 投資需要が期待通り実現すれば、投資促進策がない場合と比べて名目GDP水準を平均＋0.5％程度押し上げ

日本：原油の代替調達は継続。ただし、油価再上昇や輸送コスト増加が物価上振れ要因に
◼ 今後も原油の代替調達が継続する見通し。時期により調達先が変化するものの、原油の供給不足は回避

日本：コスト高を受け、コアCPIは26年度末にかけて加速。27年度は消費税減税が下押し要因に

― 原油高による前年比押し上げの縮小や、日銀の利上げ効果、円安一服による輸入コスト上昇圧力の緩和が影響

◼ 2026年度後半はインフレ加速により、実質賃金の伸びが鈍化。個人消費は緩慢な回復に留まる見通し
― 個人消費は、良好な所得環境にもかかわらず低調。背景には、中東情勢の悪化に伴う消費者マインドの弱含み

― 29年度の減税終了時には消費税率の再引き上げが物価を押し上げるも、新たな給付策で実質所得の落ち込みを緩和

金融政策：インフレ圧力の下で価格転嫁行動が積極化。日銀は 27年央に2％まで利上げ
◼ コスト高・供給不安定化のもと、日本企業の価格転嫁行動に積極化の兆候
― 民間調査では幅広い業種における価格転嫁短期化が示されているほか、足元では価格転嫁の動きに広がり。値上げ行
動の積極化は、収益改善を支えるとともに、物価上昇圧力の定着に繋がる可能性
◼ 物価上振れ警戒モードの金融政策運営が継続。9月会合後も日銀は短いインターバルで利上げ
― 9月会合後の会見で植田総裁は、物価引き上げから物価上振れ抑制へと「政策の局面が変化した」と説明

― 27年央にかけ四半期に1回程度のペースで利上げ。堅調な資金需要・投資計画を踏まえ、ターミナルレートは2％と予想
（注） 品目別に前年比の伸びを計算し、3カ月前から加速した品目の割合から減速した品
目の割合を差し引いて算出した指数

長期金利：市場の政策金利織り込みは2％に収束へ。長期金利は3％超の水準で高止まり
◼ 2026年度の長期金利は、物価上振れ懸念と利上げ織り込みの進展を背景に上昇
― タームプレミアム（上乗せ金利）は物価上振れ懸念から夏場にかけて大きく拡大したが、その後は緩やかな拡大ペースに
◼ 2027年度以降の長期金利は横ばい圏で推移。3％超の水準が常態化する見通し
― 物価上振れ懸念が後退するもとで、行き過ぎた利上げ織り込みは徐々に縮小
― 一方、日銀のバランスシート縮小を背景にイールドカーブの正常化が進展。日銀の大規模な国債保有で抑制されていた
タームプレミアムが緩やかに再拡大し、利上げ織り込みのはく落による金利低下圧力をおおむね相殺

（注） 政策金利織り込みは、今後10年間の政策金利に対する市場織り込みの平均。網掛け
部は期末政策金利との差分。2026年Q2までNakajima (2025)による推計値、2026年
Q3期以降みずほ総研推計値
（出所） Nakajima, J. (2025). “Impact of US monetary policy spillovers and yield curve
control policy,”、一上響・上野陽一 (2013). 「ゼロ金利下におけるタームプレミアム
の推計：日米英の長期金利の分析」、LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

為替：ドル円レートのレンジは2022年頃に円安方向へシフト。今後も構造的な円安圧力が残存
◼ 長い目でみれば、ドル円レートは2022年ごろを境にレンジが円安方向にシフト
― 2000～21年のドル円のレンジは1ドル＝80円～120円程度だったが、2022年以降は1ドル＝130円～170円程度に
― 背景には、①高い米金利、②円の需給環境の悪化、③日本のインフレノルムの定着など
◼ 先行きも、1ドル＝150円程度を中心とした円安水準が継続しやすい経済環境に
― 27年度は、短期的には「米利下げ」＋「日銀利上げ」で日米金利差が縮小し、円高要因に
― ただし、原油高が続くもとでの日本の貿易赤字、期待インフレの高止まり等が円安要因となり、大幅円高は見込まず
（出所） LSEGより、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

差から米金利が相対的に高水準

需給

• 原油高長期化で、輸入は高止まり
• デジタル赤字は拡大へ
• 対外直接投資も増加基調継続へ

• 期待インフレ率の高止まりは、購買

2026 年獲利亮眼，惟 2027 年供給壓力漸增 
 歐美航線運價優於預期，預估 3Q26 稅後 EPS 19.62 元：3Q26 營收
1,467.76 億元(+51.4%Y oY)，優於預期。受惠運價上漲獲利率提升，
然考量燃油成本占整體營業成本逾 2 成，油價上漲仍對成本形成壓力，
預估稅後淨利 424.74 億元(+95.3%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 19.62 元。 
 受地緣政治及供應鏈重組影響，預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 13.24 元：展
望後市，根據 Clarksons Aug -26 預測，2026 年全球貨櫃供給年增
4.6%，需求年增 3.8%，供給增幅仍高於需求，惟供需缺口較 2025 年
逐步收斂。預估 2026 年營收 4,735.4 億元(+24.9%Y oY)，稅後淨利
991.6 億元(+44.6%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 45.8 元。展望 2027 年，隨新船
陸續交付，儘管塞港情況仍可能限縮有效 運力，惟考量整體供給成長
仍高於需求，預期市場運價將逐步回歸常態。整體而言，預估2027 年
營收 4,097.3 億元(-13.5%YoY)，稅後淨利 482.4 億元(-51.4%Y oY)，
稅後 EPS 22.28 元。 
 維持中立評等，目標價 265 元：評價方面，以 2Q27F BVPS 元計算，
目前 PB 0.8X ，介於長榮疫情前區間 0.5-1.5X 中下緣，儘符合高股息
題 材 ， 然 隨 歐 美 傳 統 淡 季 到 來 ， 維 持 中 立 評 等 ， 目 標 價265 元
(2Q27BVPS PB 0.9X)。

元富證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市敦化南路二段 97 號 19 樓 (02)2325-3299  (115)金管投顧新字第 016 號          Page 2 of 7 
個股報告 
長榮 為 全 球 貨 櫃 運 力 第七大 航
商，市占率 5.9% 
 長榮成立於 1968 年 9 月，實收資本額 216.5 億元。截至 3Q26，全球超過 1.3
萬位員工及 293 個代理行，航線擴及 101 個國家，總船隊數共 242 艘(自有船
比例超過 6 成)，預計至 2030 年仍有 72 艘船待交付。根據 Alphaliner 統計，
截至目前長榮為全球貨櫃運力第七大航商，市佔率約 5.9%。長榮 1H26 營收
結構為貨運收入 93%，船艙租收入 1.2%，其他 5.8%。

歐 美 航 線 運 價 優 於 預 期 ， 預 估
3Q26 稅後 EPS 19.62 元 
 長榮 Sep-26 營收 497.11 億元(+1.48%MoM，+63.75%Y oY)，3Q26 營收達成
率 105.5%，優於預期，主要受惠歐美航線運價走升，且旺季附加費及燃油附
加費挹注營收 。獲利方面 ，受惠運價上漲帶動獲利率提升 ， 惟中東衝突於Jul-26
一度趨緩後再度升溫，且考量燃油成本占整體營業成本逾 2 成，油價上漲仍對
成本形成壓力，預估 3Q26 毛利率 38.91%。由於營收規模擴大，費用率有望
下降 ，預估營益率 35.71%，假設 匯率 持穩 ，預估稅後淨利 424.74 億元
(+164.9%QoQ，+95.3%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 19.62 元。

元富證券投資顧問股份有限公司 台北市敦化南路二段 97 號 19 樓 (02)2325-3299  (115)金管投顧新字第 016 號          Page 3 of 7 
個股報告 
歐美航線需求放緩 ，預估 4Q26
稅後 EPS 14.89 元 
 根據上海航交所 10/9 公告，上海出口集裝箱運價指數 (SCFI) 3,664.11 點
(+0.05%WoW，+215.76%Y oY)，隨中國十一長假結束，歐線運價短期反彈，
分別為遠東-歐洲線+3.41%WoW，遠東-地中海線+0.2%WoW；美線持平，遠
東-美西線+0.08%WoW，遠東-美東-0.04%WoW。觀察 10/11 公告跨大西洋、
歐 洲 及 跨 太 平 洋 航 線 運 力 分 別 變 動 -1.14%WoW 、 -1.88%WoW 及
+0.48%WoW。整體而言，隨歐美航線逐步進入傳統淡季，航商陸續調整運力
配置，其中歐洲線運力投放減少，美線則大致持平，顯示航商持續透過運力調
節支撐市場運價。

展望 4Q26，需求面而言，歐線隨傳統旺季結束，終端需求動能轉弱，預期後
續運價將呈緩步回落走勢。美線方面，旺季需求已近尾聲，且部分船班裝載率
已有下滑，儘管 Sep-26 川習會後美國調降部分消費品關稅，包括玩具、小型
家電及節慶裝飾品等，惟目前尚未出現明顯大型備貨潮。供給面而言，隨水情
陸續改善 ， 巴拿馬運河管理局預計自2026/10/15 起將每日允許通行量由 32 艘
回升至 33 艘，惟仍低於原先 36 艘水準。考量大型貨櫃船通行具一定優先性，
目前對船期營運影響相對有限，惟通行量尚未完全恢復，仍可能使美東線有效
運力有所收斂。另，美伊衝突及紅海通行風險仍存， Gemini 聯盟持續恢復部
分紅海航線，近期亦傳出 Premier Alliance 有望於 4Q26 恢復部分紅海航線，
若後續其他航商陸續跟進，繞航所吸收之有效運力恐逐步釋放，對市場運價形
成壓力。

整體而言，儘管近洋線進入傳統旺季，惟長榮歐美航線營收占比較高，預期整
體運價及貨量將隨淡季到來而回落 ， 預估4Q26 營收 1,350.87 億元(-8%QoQ，
+57.6%Y oY)。獲利方面，儘管油價仍維持高位，惟隨貨量減少，相關變動成
本亦有望下降，預估毛利率 37.9%。另考量長榮往年於 4Q 提列年終獎金費
用，預期費用率將季增，預估營益率 29.4%。假設匯率持穩，預估稅後淨利
323.53 億元(-23.8%QoQ，+279.8%Y oY)，4Q26 稅後 EPS 14.94 元。

2026 年有效運力供給受限 ， 預估
稅後 EPS 45.75 元 
 展望後市，根據 Clarksons Aug-26 預測，2026 年全球貨櫃供給年增 4.6%，需
求年增 3.8%，供給增幅仍高於需求，惟供需缺口較 2025 年逐步收斂。短期而
言，受到缺工及天候因素影響，部分港口作業效率下降，近期塞港現象增加，
有助吸收部分有效運力。展望 2027 年，在尚未考慮紅海全面復航之前提下，
Clarksons 預估全球貨櫃供給+8.8%Y oY，需求+3.5%YoY，供需缺口將再度擴
大；若紅海航線進一步恢復，繞航所吸收之有效運力亦將逐步釋放，供給壓力
恐進一步增加。需求方面，則須持續關注停戰協議進展、美國關稅政策變化及
通膨壓力對終端需求之影響。

展望 2026 年，長榮持續優化航線布局，強化東南亞、中東、非洲及中南美洲
等區域配置 ， 並透過長約布局降低市場運價波動影響，整體而言 ，元富預估 2026
年營收 4,735.4 億元(+24.9%Y oY)。獲利方面，預估毛利率 30.8%，營益率
25.4%，稅後淨利 991.6 億元(+44.6%Y oY)，預估 2026 年稅後 EPS 45.8 元。
展望 2027 年，隨新船陸續交付，全球貨櫃船隊運力持續增加，儘管塞港情況
仍可能限縮有效運力，惟考量整體供給成長仍高於需求，預期市場運價將逐步
回歸常態。整體而言，預估 2027 年營收 4,097.3 億元(-13.5%Y oY)，稅後淨利
482.4 億元(-51.4%Y oY)，稅後 EPS 22.28 元。

營收走勢圖

研究員/王建智 MICHAELWANG82121188@UNI-
PSG.COM

研究部對於長榮看法轉趨正向，係因：(1) 3Q26 受惠貨櫃航運
旺季及運價回升，平均運價漲幅明顯高於燃油成本增幅， 帶動獲
利顯著成長。(2) 4Q26 雖進入傳統淡季，惟北美農曆春節前備
貨 、歐洲貨量成長及亞洲區域航線旺季提供支撐，預期獲利雖低
於 3Q26，但仍優於 2Q26。(3) 2027 年雖面臨新船交付增加的供
給壓力， 但全球碼頭擴建速度落後船隊成長，加上塞港及紅海繞
航持續消耗有效運力，有助減緩新增供給對運價的衝擊， 北美及
歐洲航線長約運價亦預期高於 2026 年，支撐全年獲利。預估
2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 分別為45.21 元（+42.7%YoY）／27.89 元
（-38.3%YoY）。歷史本淨比區間為 0.4～1.8 倍，以 2027 年底
預估每股淨值 298.36 元為評價基礎，給予0.95 倍本淨比， 目標
價 283 元，將投資評等調升至買進。

重點摘要： 
1. 3Q26 運價漲幅高於燃油成本增幅，帶動獲利顯著成長 
長榮 3Q26 營收 1,467.76 億元，+39.6%QoQ/+51.4%YoY，主要受
惠第三季貨櫃航運傳統旺季， 歐美航線進入零售商備貨高峰， 帶
動貨運需求回升，加上紅海繞航、 全球港口壅塞持續消耗有效運
力，以及航商透過艙位控管調節供給，推升主要航線運價。7、8
月平均運費分別為 1,539 及1,577 美元／TEU， 參考9 月SCFI 月
均 3,657 點，+8.0%MoM， 推估9 月平均運費約 1,703 美元／TEU，
並假設 9 月貨量與 8 月持平，則 3Q26 平均運費約 1,606 美元／
TEU，+44.7%QoQ，貨量276 萬 TEU，-1.8%QoQ，營收成長主要由
運價上漲貢獻。

成本方面 ， 預估每噸耗油成本由2Q26 的 645 美元升至 714 美元 ，
+10.6%QoQ，假設季度耗油量持平，燃油費用增加約22 億元，惟
運價漲幅明顯高於燃油成本增幅 ， 營收增加帶來的獲利貢獻可望
大幅超過成本上升影響。預估 3Q26 稅後淨利 479.69 億元，
+199.2%QoQ/+120.6%YoY，稅後EPS 22.16 元。

### Page 2

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  2

圖一、各月平均運費、每噸耗油成本、貨量

資料來源：長榮，統一投顧整理 
2. 4Q26 進入傳統淡季，惟區域航線需求支撐營運 
第四季為貨櫃航運傳統淡季， 整體需求不如第三季旺季強勁， 惟
根據訂艙及裝載情況 ， 至11 月前貨量仍具支撐 。 北美航線方面 ，
美國主要港口進口貨櫃量維持高檔，零售庫存銷售比低於 1.3，
顯示零售商庫存水位相對偏低，仍有持續補貨需求，預期 11 月
貨量隨淡季趨緩 ，12 月則有農曆春節前備貨需求支撐 。 歐洲航線
方面 ，2026 年貨量成長優於預期 ， 雖運價自7 月後逐步回落 ， 但
紅海地緣政治風險尚未解除， 繞航仍持續消耗有效運力。 亞洲區
域航線第四季進入傳統旺季 ， 受惠中國加一帶動東南亞及南亞原
料、半成品運輸需求成長。此外，全球塞港造成約300 萬至 400
萬 TEU 有效運力損失，占全球總運力約 11%至 12%，搭配航商艙
位控管，有助支撐淡季運價。

預估 4Q26 營收 1,174.21 億元，-20.0%QoQ/+37.0%YoY，稅後淨
利 255.68 億元，-46.7%QoQ/+200.2%YoY，稅後 EPS 11.81 元。

3. 2027 年新船交付壓力增加，港口壅塞限制有效運力供給 
2027~2029 年為全球貨櫃船新船交付高峰，目前全球貨櫃船總運
力約3,400 萬TEU，至2030 年待交付新船運力超過1,400 萬TEU，
主流研調機構(Clarksons、Drewry)預估 2027 年名目運力供給增
速為 7.7~7.9%，供給成長壓力仍是 2027 年主要風險。惟名目運
力增加不代表有效運力等幅成長 ， 過去十年全球碼頭處理能力年
均成長約 2.7%，低於船隊運力年均成長約 5.4%，港口基礎設施
擴張速度落後船隊成長，塞港問題短期難以改善。紅海及蘇伊士
運河恢復通行亦為重要變數， 歐洲航線若恢復通行，單程約節省

### Page 3

請參見末頁重要批露及免責聲明  3

7 天 、 往返約14 天 ， 但船舶集中抵港可能加劇碼頭壅塞 ， 抵銷部
分航程縮短釋出的運力。長榮截至 2030 年尚有 72 艘新船待交
付 ， 合計運力約89 萬 TEU， 並持續投資重要樞紐碼頭 ， 提升靠泊
效率及船隊調度彈性。

4. 2027 年歐美長約運價預期高於 2026 年，支撐全年獲利 
2027 年北美及歐洲航線長約運價預期均高於 2026 年，主要受惠
三項因素 。 首先 ，2026 年第三季貨櫃運價大幅上漲 ， 目前現貨運
價明顯高於前一年度長約議價時的水準 ， 為新年度合約價格調升
奠定基礎 。 其次 ， 全球港口處理能力擴張速度落後船隊運力成長 ，
加上紅海繞航及主要港口壅塞持續消耗有效運力 ， 航商供給調節
能力提升，有利維持長約議價能力。最後，歐美航線貨運需求仍
具支撐 ， 北美零售商庫存水位偏低 ， 歐洲航線貨量成長優於預期 ，
提供長約價格上調條件。歐洲航線新約自 2027 年初開始適用，
北美航線則自 5 月起生效 ， 預期新年度長約運價調升將逐步反映
於營收，並有助減緩現貨運價波動對獲利的影響，支撐 2027 年
營運表現。

5. 預估 2026/2027 年稅後 EPS 45.21/27.89 元，塞港及長約運
價調升支撐獲利高於歷史平均水準 
預估 2026/2027 年營收分別為 4,558.69／4,178.70 億元，分別
+20.3%YoY/-8.3%YoY，稅後淨利分別為 978.74／603.81 億元，
分別+42.7%YoY/-38.3%YoY，稅後 EPS 分別為 45.21 元／27.89
元。2026 年受惠第三季運價大幅上漲 ， 帶動全年獲利回升 。2027
年雖面臨新船交付增加及運價自高檔回落的壓力 ， 惟全球碼頭處
理能力成長落後船隊運力，加上塞港及紅海繞航限制有效供給，
歐美航線長約價格亦預期高於 2026 年 ， 有助減緩獲利下滑幅度 。

附錄 一 ： 評 等 之 標 準 
評等  定義  
強力買進     預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬超過 25%以上  
買進  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬 10% 以上  
中立  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 10～-10%  
降低持股  預估未來 6 個月內的絕對報酬介於 (-10%)以下  
未評等  沒有足夠的基本資料判斷該公司評等

資 標 的 ， 或 文 中 所 討 論 或 建 議 之 投 資 策 略 ， 投 資 人 應 就 其 是 否 適 合 本 身 財 務 狀 況 與
投 資 條 件 ， 進 一 步 諮 詢 財 務 顧 問 的 意 見 。 本 投 資 報 告 之 內 容 取 材 自 據 信 為 可 靠 之 資

### Page 1

Taiwan Information Technology

What’s new: We preview ITEQ’s 3Q26 results (due on 6 November). In our 
previous note, “Upgrading: strong beat in 2Q26 with improving outlook” (7 
August), we had underestimated the magnitude and durability of price hikes 
for E-glass and M4-and-below CCL. We raise our 2026-28E earnings by 
43-70% to reflect better-than-expected pricing and gross margin 
performance, which leads to our higher TP of TWD1,110 (from TWD600).

What’s the impact: Ongoing price hike of E-glass and M4-and-below 
CCL. This week, a major Japanese copper-clad laminate (CCL) vendor 
raised its entry-level CCL price by 200% to catch up with other global 
peers’ price hike level. One of the Chinese peers is also having a constant 
c.10% ASP hike on a monthly basis in 2026. We are increasingly confident 
in the management’s target for its blended price hike in 2H26. We now 
forecast c.30%/20% price hikes in 3Q26E and 4Q26E, respectively (vs. our 
previous estimates of 20%/10%). Looking ahead, we expect another 10% 
price hike per quarter in 1H27 (c.20% price hike in total), while pricing 
should gradually stabilise in 2H27 given the rising supply of E-glass, in our 
view. While the visibility towards 2H27 and 2028 remains relatively limited, 
we believe the key upside risks to our current forecast will be the stronger-
than-expected price hike magnitude and durability of the current up-cycle.

3Q26 results preview. After factoring in stronger-than-expected price 
hikes, ITEQ’s 3Q26 revenue grew c.35% QoQ, mainly driven by the 30% 
price hike despite utilisation rate staying at c.75%. We lift our gross margin 
forecast to c.30.3% (from 24.7% in our August note), in line with the August 
preliminary results’ pre-tax margin of 25.8%. With price hikes continuing, 
we expect the 4Q26E gross margin to expand to c.35%. Given disciplined 
capacity expansion through 2028, we expect the higher GM to be sustained 
through 2028. Beyond price hike, we believe the key longer-term growth 
drivers include: a) rising regular server demand and new platform adoption 
(using M7 grade CCL) in 2027; and b) M9 certification by more clients.

What we recommend: We raise our 2026-28E EPS by 43-70% to factor in 
the price hikes. We reaffirm our Buy (1) rating, and raise our 12M TP to 
TWD1,110 (from TWD600), based on an unchanged target PER of 22x 
(higher than the range during the past 12 months of 5-18x) applied to our 1-
year-forward EPS. While we expect the current upcycle will eventually ease 
starting from mid-2027, we remain positive on the near-term price outlook. 
Key downside risk: weaker-than-expected regular server demand.

How we differ: Our 2026-28E EPS are 36-60% above the Bloomberg 
consensus, mainly due to our more aggressive view on ITEQ’s price hikes 
and margin profile.

Company profile 
ITEQ Corporation is a leading manufacturer of high performance copper clad laminate materials used 
for the fabrication of printed circuit boards and was founded in April of 1997. ITEQ’s products find use in 
many different application areas, such as computational and communications applications, Radio 
Frequency and Microwave devices, Automotive applications, and High Density Interconnect solutions.

ESG analysis

Update Date: 14 Oct 2026 
Source: Daiwa, Company

ESG risks
Risks Management Analyst comments
G
Executive/board
quality 2
All board members implement the company’s operation faithfully and fulfil their obligations with
discipline and care. Independent directors obey related laws and company regulations to protect
the rights of the company and stockholders.
Capital management 2
ITEQ paid a cash DPS of TWD3 in 2025, representing a 72% payout ratio. We believe ITEQ will
sustain a similar payout level of c.75% over 2026-28E. We see its balance between investment
and dividend as appropriate.
Related party &
transaction 2 ITEQ’s sales to related parties were insignificant versus its total revenue.
S Supply chainmanagement 2 ITEQ requires all major raw material suppliers to comply with ESG, ethics, and regulatorystandards. Annual RBA audits were conducted for vendors, all rated A–C.
S Data security 1 ITEQ has established an ISO/IEC 27001-certified Information Security Management System,conducts quarterly training, and implements system recovery drills to ensure risk preparedness.
Note: Management score represents a company's ability to manage/benefit from certain ESG topics. The scores range from 1 to 3, with 1 being the strongest.

### Page 3

TSMC (2330.TW)
15 October 2026 Citi Research
3
Rather than now slowing down, it’s actually getting stronger
TSMC reported an upbeat 3Q26 result with robust 4Q26 outlook. It guided for
4Q26 revenue of US$52.3–54.3 billion, representing about 14% QoQ growth and
58% year-over-year growth at the midpoint (based on FX at NT$31.8 per USD).
4Q26 GM/OPM is expected at 65–67% and 56.5–58.5%. TSMC reiterated its 2026
capex to be US$60–64 billion and said investment from 2026 to 2028 will be

significantly higher than in the previous three years, reflecting strong multi-year
customer demand, including agentic AI. It is also worth noting that TSMC plans to
begin using the High-NA EUV technology around 2030 and develop a 12-inch
mask capability by 2031, when it expects the tools to offer sufficient maturity and
cost effectiveness. Management said this timing does not undermine its
confidence in technology leadership.
Management described AI-related demand as exceptionally strong, supported by

accelerators, networking chips, and rising demand for CPUs associated with
agentic AI. TSMC’s previously guided AI GPU/accelerator CAGR to be mid-to-high
50%, but now sees more upside ahead. Given rising concern on AI expanding
peaking out, the management said it is clear that its capacity planning strategy has
considered customer forecasts, data-center construction, power availability, and
other supply-chain constraints. Management still judges demand to be above

available supply and is working to close the gap, while emphasizing disciplined
planning to limit the risk of overbuilding.
Figure 1. TSMC – 3Q26 results comparison
© 2026 Citigroup Inc. No redistribution without Citigroup’s written permission.
Source: Citi Research, Bloomberg
Capacity expansion plan and allocation
During the earnings call, TSMC also mentioned that despite the tight capacity
supply, customers cannot generally sublet or auction wafer capacity, while there is

limited case that collaboration is through mask-license agreements. For the
advanced packaging, TSMC has announced its Global Foundries and VIS’
agreement on interposer as part of its backend supply strategy, intended to help
ensure customers have enough capacity to bring products to market; It also
reiterated that TSMC’s CoWoS remains as the leading customer’s choice and is
already in volume production at 5x reticle size, while it is developing 9x and larger

solutions and evaluating CoPoS.

### Page 4

TSMC (2330.TW)
15 October 2026 Citi Research
4
Reiterate Buy, best position in AI arena
Our earnings projection is largely unchanged for 2027 /2028 earnings. While its
capex expansion, overseas operation and N2 will likely be a margin dilution factor
initially, due to its cross-node leverage, high UTR and yield rate, we believe its GM
downside is manageable. Overall, we see strong advanced node demand into next
year and the long-term order visibility will be supported for higher capex cycle.  We

maintain our TP to NT$4,000, based on unchanged 25x of 2027 EPS estimate;
reiterate Buy.
Key TSMC earnings call recap
n4Q26 guidance
–Sales: US$52.3-54.3bn (+14% QoQ at the midpoint)
–GPM: 65-67% (assuming USDTWD=31.8)
–OPM: 56.5-58.5%
n2026 full-year & long-term guidance
–Full year sales: raise 2026 full-year USD-based sales guidance from more
than 40% growth to +44% growth
–Full year capex: retain its 2026 capex guidance at US$60-64bn (70-80%
advanced process, 10% specialty, 10-20% for advanced packaging, testing

and masks)
–GM outlook
•4Q GM to be impacted by N2 and overseas fab ramps, but partially
supported by productivity improvement, UTR enhancement and
optimizing capacity allocation
•GM dilution from overseas fab would be c2ppts impact for the full-year
2026; with more new capacity expansion in the US, the margin dilution
impact would widen to 3-4ppts in the next few years.
–Long-term outlook
•Retains 5-year AI sales CAGR (2024-2029) remains at high-50% or above

nCapacity planning
–TSMC currently has 20 fabs in total. It plans to add 13 fabs in Taiwan and add
5-6 overseas fabs.
–N2 capacity will double in 2027; it has also started preparing for A14 capacity
with volume production starting from 2028.
–US Arizona fab 2 construction is complete and begin volume production in
3Q27, ahead of schedule.
–JP fab 2 would produce N3 with mass production from 2028

### Page 10

TSMC (2330.TW)
15 October 2026 Citi Research
10
TSMC
Company description
TSMC is the founder and leader of the dedicated IC foundry segment. The
company has built its reputation by offering advanced and "more-than-
Moore" wafer production processes and industry-leading manufacturing
efficiency. From its inception in 1987, TSMC has consistently offered the
foundry segment's leading technologies and TSMC-compatible design
services.
Investment strategy

We rate TSMC shares as Buy. We expect TSMC to deliver sales growth of
>40% in recent years thanks to strong growth from HPC/AI as well as stable
demand growth from smartphone, Io T, and automotive on tech migration and
a shortened replacment cycle. CPU outsourcing to TSMC offers further
growth potential. A rising cash dividend should also support the share price.
While TSMC has been increasing its capex meaningfully since 2009, its cost
structure remaisn competitive through continuous product mix

improvements, efficiency enhancements, and cost reductions.
Valuation
Our target price for TSMC shares of NT$4,000 is based on 25x our 2027E
EPS, higher than its three-year average forward PER, versus global peers’
average of 16-25x. We believe our target PER is justified by TSMC's leading
position in advanced process node and robust AI demand growth outlook.
Given its continuing leadership in the semiconductor foundry industry, we
believe TSMC’s stable order visibility and earnings outlook make it less

vulnerable to a potential global economic downturn than some of its peers.
Our target price equates to 2026E/27E P/E of ~ 36x/25x and 2026E/27E P/B of
~13x/10x.
Risks
Key downside risks that could impede the shares from achieving our target
price include: 1) weakness in the global semiconductor market; 2) larger-
than-expected margin contractions due to depreciation cost increases and
strong NTD; 3) competitors entering the foundry business; 4) longer-than-

expected digestion of inventory in the supply chain; and 5) a slowdown in
demand either due to a global economic downturn or a global trade
disruption triggered by tariffs.

Guide to Citi Research Fundamental Research Investment Ratings:
Citi Research stock recommendations include an investment rating and an optional risk rating to highlight high risk stocks.

Risk rating takes into account both price volatility and fundamental criteria. Stocks will either have no risk rating or a High risk
rating assigned.
Investment Ratings: Citi Research investment ratings are Buy, Neutral and Sell. Our ratings are a function of analyst
expectations of expected total return ("ETR") and risk. ETR is the sum of the forecast price appreciation (or depreciation) plus
the dividend yield for a stock within the next 12 months.  The target price is based on a 12 month time horizon. The Investment

rating definitions are: Buy (1) ETR of 15% or more or 25% or more for High risk stocks; and Sell (3) for negative ETR. Any covered
stock not assigned a Buy or a Sell is a Neutral (2). For stocks rated Neutral (2), if an analyst believes that there are insufficient
valuation drivers and/or investment catalysts to derive a positive or negative investment view, they may elect with the approval
of Citi Research management not to assign a target price and, thus, not derive an ETR. Citi Research may suspend its rating

and target price and assign “Rating Suspended” status for regulatory and/or internal policy reasons. Citi Research may also
suspend its rating and target price and assign "Under Review" status for other exceptional circumstances (e.g. lack of
information critical to the analyst's thesis, trading suspension) affecting the company and/or trading in the company's
securities. In both such situations, the rating and target price will show as “—“ and “-“ respectively in the rating history price

chart. Prior to 11 April 2022 Citi Research assigned “Under Review” status to both situations and prior to 11 Nov 2020 only in
exceptional circumstances. As soon as practically possible, the analyst will publish a note re-establishing a rating and
investment thesis. Investment ratings are determined by the ranges described above at the time of initiation of coverage, a
change in investment and/or risk rating, or a change in target price (subject to limited management discretion). At times, the

expected total returns may fall outside of these ranges because of market price movements and/or other short-term volatility
or trading patterns. Such interim deviations will be permitted but will become subject to review by Research Management.
Your decision to buy or sell a security should be based upon your personal investment objectives and should be made only
after evaluating the stock's expected performance and risk.
Catalyst Watch/Short Term Views (“STV”) Ratings Disclosure:

Catalyst Watch and STV Upside/Downside calls: Citi Research may also include a Catalyst Watch or STV Upside or Downside
call to indicate the analyst expects the share price to rise (fall) in absolute terms over a specified period of 30 or 90 days in
reaction to one or more specific near-term catalysts or events impacting the company or the market. A Catalyst Watch will be
published when Analyst confidence is high that an impact to share price will occur; it will be a STV when confidence level is

moderate. A Catalyst Watch or STV Upside/Downside call will automatically expire at the end of the specified 30/90 day
period. The Catalyst Watch will also be automatically removed if share price performance (calculated at market close) exceeds
15% against the direction of the call (unless over-ridden by the analyst). The analyst may also remove a Catalyst Watch or STV
call prior to the end of the specified period in a published research note. A Catalyst Watch/STV Upside or Downside call may be

our ratings distribution table for our Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside rating system. However, we reiterate that we do
not consider No View to be a recommendation. For all Catalyst Watch/STV Upside/Downside calls, risk exists that the
catalyst(s) and associated share-price movement will not materialize as expected.

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) 발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다HD현대중공업(329180) 발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung

### Page 1

00

HD 현대중공업 (329180/KS) 
발전 엔진으로 또 성장한다 
기다렸던 4 행정 중속 엔진 캐파 증설 단행 
HD현대중공업의 4행정 엔진 캐파는 기존 3.0GW에서 증설이 완료되는 '28년부터 
램프업을 거쳐 '30년 7.2GW까지 확대될 계획이다. ①[육상 발전용]은 신공장(울산 
온산) 3GW 와 HD 현대엔진(전남 영암) 1GW 를 합쳐 4.0GW, ②[선박용]은 
2.3GW에서 3.2GW로 확대된다. HD현대엔진 캐파는 기존 0.7GW에서 1GW까
지 확대되며, 해당 라인은 신공장이 가동되기 전 ‘27~’28 년 구간에서의 선박&육상 엔
진 모두 대응 가능하다. 신공장 캐파는 총 3.0GW로, ‘28년 약 30%의 가동률로 시
작해 램프업을 거쳐 ‘30 년부터 풀캐파로 20MW급 모델을 생산할 예정이다. 4 행정 
중속엔진에 대한 수요 가시성은 높다. 동사가 공유했던 육상 발전 엔진 인콰이어리 규
모는 기존 약 3~9GW였으나, 현재는 이를 크게 상회하는 수준으로 소통하고 있다.

최근 부유식 데이터센터(FDC)에 대한 하이퍼스케일러들의 인콰이어리 또한 확대되고 
있다. HD 한국조선해양은 최근 미국 선급(ABS)로부터 100MW급 규모의 부유식 발
전 설비(BMPP)에 대한 기본 인증(AiP)를 획득했으며, 동사는 FDC 신조 사업과 더불
어 발전원 4 행정 엔진을 파워십 및 부유식 발전설비(BMPP)에 납품하며 그룹사들과 
패키지 사업으로 진행할 예정이다. 가시화된 수요를 기반으로 엔진기계사업부의 구조적 
성장 보여줄 것으로 전망하며, 이에 따른 리레이팅을 예상한다.

투자의견 ‘매수’ 및 목표주가 기존 97 만원 유지 
HD 현대중공업에 대한 투자의견 ‘매수’ 및 목표주가 기존 97 만원을 유지한다. 동사는 
올해 상선수주 목표 이미 초과달성하며 안정적인 펀더멘털 유지 중이다. 하반기 남은 
기간은 LNGC, VLGC/VLAC 중심의 선별 수주 전략을 통해 ‘28 년 이후의 상선 부
문 수익성 유지 충분히 가능할 것으로 예상된다. 글로벌 지정학적 리스크 확대에 따른 
해군 현대화 사이클 기조에 맞추어 미 해군은 동맹국의 호위함 구매를 고려하고 있으
며, MASGA를 기반으로 중장기적으로 미국 전투함을 건조하기 위해 HD현대의 미국 
진출 가능성 높다는 판단이다. 또한 미국을 제외한 글로벌 함정(페루, 필리핀, 말레이시
아, 칠레, 사우디, 이집트 등) 수주 파이프라인도 다수 보유하고 있어 함정 모멘텀에 따
른 리레이팅 충분히 가능할 전망이다. 특수선+4 행정 엔진+FDC+SMR 등 조선소에 
붙일 수 있는 모든 추가 성장동력을 보유한 유일한 종목이기에 조선업 최선호주(Top-
Pick)로 제시한다. 최근 불거지고 있는 노조 파업 문제 해소될 시 저점 매수 기회라는 
판단이다.

2026-09-17

COMPLIANCE NOTICE 
작성자(한승한)는 본 조사분석자료에 게재된 내용들이 본인의 의견을 정확하게 반영하고 있으며, 외부의 부당한 압력이나 간섭없이 신의성실하게 작성되었음을 확인합니다. 
본 보고서에 언급된 종목의 경우 당사 조사분석담당자는 본인의 담당종목을 보유하고 있지 않습니다. 
본 보고서는 기관투자가 또는 제 3 자에게 사전 제공된 사실이 없습니다. 
당사는 자료공표일 현재 해당기업과 관련하여 특별한 이해 관계가 없습니다. 
종목별 투자의견은 다음과 같습니다. 
투자판단 3 단계(6 개월기준) 15%이상 → 매수 / -15%~15% → 중립 / -15%미만 → 매도

### Page 1

CHIEF INVESTMENT OFFICE
Capital Market Outlook
September 14, 2026
All data, projections and opinions are as of the date of this report and subject to change.
IN THIS ISSUE
Macro Strategy—Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade:
Rapid advances in Artificial Intelligence (AI) have prompted prominent architects of
the new economy to conclude the Singularity is arriving ahead of schedule as signs
of its effects proliferate in the multitude of new AI models running in the increasing

number of data centers driving one of the biggest investment booms in U.S. history.
Superintelligence that surpasses collective human intelligence has long been one of the
predictions of futurists analyzing the implications of the Singularity. Superabundance is
one of the results of Superintelligence predicted by those who have written about the
consequences of this major acceleration in technological innovation. The early arrival of
these radical forces for change poses imminent challenges for society and investors that

will shape the next decade in critical ways.
In particular, the heightened uncertainty about how this new era plays out increases the
utility of a well-diversified approach to wealth management in our view.
Market View—Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite: The recent
rise in Treasury yields reflects a confluence of factors, including Federal Reserve (Fed)
policy uncertainty, deteriorating fiscal dynamics, resilient economic growth, persistent

inflation concerns and a record wave of debt issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate borrowers, particularly in the long end of the curve. Importantly, we view this
as a period of market adjustment rather than a cause for alarm.
Episodes of debt pressures can be a natural consequence of elevated supply, in addition
to other external factors and could be resolved through higher yields that help balance
supply and demand. While interest rate volatility may remain elevated and long-term

yields could settle at structurally higher levels, today's combination of attractive nominal
and real yields presents a potential opportunity for investors to extend beyond cash and
short-duration holdings and move closer to strategic duration targets.

MACRO STRATEGY
Superintelligence and Superabundance in the Next Decade
Chief Investment Office, Macro Strategy Team
While some academics have claimed the Singularity is still off in the distant future, or even
unlikely at all, those on the front lines of AI model development have recently asserted that it
has already begun. For example, Elon Musk has pointed to recent issues with AI models going

outside their proscribed limits, including hacking external systems and completing previously
unsolved math problems. Over a year ago, Sam Altman wrote, "We are past the event horizon; the
takeoff has started. Humanity is close to building superintelligence..." In short, the rapid evolution
of these models has convinced knowledgeable observers that the Singularity has begun.
Superintelligence is the primary driver of the Singularity as exponential advances in AI ultimately

create an artificial Superintelligence that is much smarter and faster than the collective
intelligence of humanity. As such, it can solve practical problems that remain unsolved by humans
and do it in a timely fashion. Superabundance is the logical implication of Superintelligence
as it works to create a world where economic goods, services and energy are essentially free
because of the omniscience implied by the most efficient processing of unlimited information by

superintelligent models. In essence, Superintelligence could mimic the Creation.
Some aspects of Superabundance include automated production of desired output that
eliminates most physical labor, clean energy and synthetic materials that eliminate resource
scarcity, and increased longevity as Superintelligence is applied to maintaining physical health
via, for example, synthetic organ replacement and personalized illness treatments. Obviously,
Superabundance pulls the rug out from under traditional economics, which is the study of how

scarce resources are allocated. While the "scarcity" vanishes in this new world, the allocation issue
remains and is likely to become the focus of political debate. In particular, the question of how
jobs and incomes fit into a superabundant world is likely to become a bigger and bigger issue.
The trend of technology replacing more and more of the labor share of income is already decades
old and dates back to the beginnings of digital technology. That trend has picked up speed

with the increasing profit share of output the new technologies created. The ratio of income
to the number of workers is orders of magnitude higher for the big new technology companies
compared to the old economy companies of the past. This means the owners of capital
(shareholders) are receiving a bigger share of the economic pie as high-margin, high-productivity
companies based in the new technology become a bigger part of the economy. This trend is

accelerating. That is a direct implication of the movement toward and through the Singularity.
It means the issue of income inequality, which has always been on society's radar screen, will
become bigger in the years ahead unless there are wise policies to share the superabundance
of the near future. Some countries are better prepared for this future than others. Norway,
for example, has built a sovereign wealth fund (SWF) over the past 25 years that represents a

substantial share of capital income for each of its citizens. In the U.S., with its widespread equity
culture, thanks to Individual Retirement Accounts and 401K plans, capital income is more widely
distributed compared to Europe with its much more debt-based capital markets. In any event,
widespread equity ownership is one way to deal with a superabundant economy where AI has
significantly reduced the labor share of income.
Demographics add to the natural evolution from labor income to capital income as societies

become wealthier. Aging societies of retirees by definition depend on capital income more than
labor income. This transition of the economy to higher margins and faster earnings growth is
the fortunate accompaniment to the need for more income from capital as societies become
older. The longevity trend naturally accelerates this process, which is the logical result of
technological progress raising productivity and living standards. People live longer in wealthy

societies, as subsistence constraints on life spans diminish while lifestyle and mental health
constraints become more the issue. How lifestyles and mental health evolve in a world where
work becomes less necessary is already showing signs of their increased importance for the
future of public health.
The current accelerating profits cycle reflects this impact of new technology on the relative
returns to labor and capital. The trend so far has been relatively slow compared to what's

coming if Superintelligence and Superabundance are the outcomes the Singularity promises as
increasingly seems likely. Because much financial analysis relies on past cycles the accelerating
Investment Implications
Rapid innovation, political upheaval
and increasing creative destruction
raise the difficulty of valuing
longer duration assets as we saw
earlier this year in the software
space. Higher-risk premia are a
likely result. On balance, this favors

Value-oriented assets over Growth.

spending environment reinforce this
preference.

### Page 3

3 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
profits and rising margins of the current economy have naturally focused attention on what
happens when conditions revert to the mean of lower margins and more historically normal
profit growth. Looked at through this lens, Equities appear overvalued and destined to correct
bubble-like conditions. The Singularity, however, implies "this time is different," the four most
dangerous words in investing. Yet, the slowly evolving shift toward capital income already under

way is an early warning sign of bigger things to come as these trends are turbo-charged to warp
speed while the economy passes through the Singularity. Seen this way, a super-cycle in profits,
margins and stock values seems logical.
A revamping of the social safety net to address the conditions implied by Superabundance
is already a hot topic in Silicon Valley. Various proposals for a universal basic income (UBI)
are being bandied about. Typically, UBI refers to the government giving everyone an income

sufficient to meet some living standard. This automatically implies a big role for government in
the new economy. Given the fourth-turning breakdown in the political process and governance,
alternatives that avoid the government sector might also be considered. For example, another
implication of the Singularity is an accelerating accumulation of wealth fueling a growing supply
of altruistic and philanthropic potential that arguably could be administered more efficiently by

the private sector than the government where widespread allegations of corrupt administration
at all levels have proliferated since the pandemic. Given the growing wealth pie and the diversity
of social priorities among its owners addressing more social needs through philanthropy is a
likely option for the superabundant economy. In any event, the relative roles of government and
the private sector are necessarily an issue to be hashed out in this revolutionary time.

More glimmers of the UBI-like movements have popped up recently. As mentioned, Norway's
SWF is an example of how a well-administered government has been preparing for this future.
In the U.S. government sponsored tax-advantaged savings accounts from birth have been
instituted with private donations being added to the program. Governments, including the U.S.,
have increasingly been buying stakes in private investments for various purposes with the result

that the focus solely on sovereign debt can be a misleading indicator of fiscal fragility.
Japan is a significant example of the difference between gross government debt and net
government debt after subtracting government owned assets. Its gross debt is around 279% of
its gross domestic product (GDP) which is much higher than any other major economy. However,
Japan government assets total around 192% of GDP , including domestic and foreign Equities,

bonds, and loans. As a result, its net debt is less than 100% of GDP and more in line with other
major economies. As one of the most aged societies in the world, Japan points the way toward
how societies can cope with longevity. Government ownership of a broad array of higher yielding
global assets funded with low sovereign debt interest rates appears to be a path the U.S.
Treasury is also starting to pursue in managing its debt burden.
The main risks to the uptopian promise of Superabundance are the dystopian possibilities

that would follow from poor social decision-making. Around the world, different governments
will follow different approaches to navigating the unprecedented challenges posed by the
Singularity. As the U.S. celebrates its 250-year anniversary, it is in the midst of its fourth "fourth
turning." These are the intense political upheaval periods that demographers and historians have
documented in every fourth generation. The Revolutionary War, the Civil War, and the Great

Depression and World War II mark the first three "fourth turnings." The Singularity is a major
marker for the current fourth turning.
The relative success of the U.S. in transitioning through the previous major turning points in
history provides hope for a utopian rather than a dystopian outcome. Other countries have not
transited these challenges so well for such an extended period which helps explain how the
U.S. has become the most prosperous country on earth. Nevertheless, past performance is no

guarantee of future success. As always, each generation faces new challenges to overcome, or not.
For investors, the Singularity seems to be behind an unusually strong earnings cycle that could
potentially last much longer than past history would suggest. That's positive for Equities and
risk assets in general. The extra risks associated with this rapid transformation argue for well
diversified holdings as creative destruction on steroids seems a plausible outcome in a world of

warp speed change.

MARKET VIEW
Elevated Debt Supply Is Testing the Market's Appetite
Chief Investment Office, Fixed Income Strategy Team
For much of the past two decades, rising debt levels appeared to carry few
consequences. Federal deficits expanded and corporate borrowing increased, yet
Treasury yields generally trended lower and credit spreads stayed remarkably tame.
Today, the backdrop may be changing.

Federal borrowing needs continue to grow as deficit spending remains elevated. Net
interest expense has surpassed $1 trillion annually and now rivals total U.S. defense
spending. On this front there appears no end in sight as the Congressional Budget
Office projects that the fiscal 2026 shortfall will widen to roughly $2.1 trillion. Investors
are increasingly focused on not only the level of debt outstanding, but also the pace at
which new debt is being issued—with some questioning about the sustainability of this

situation over the longer term.
This phenomenon is not limited to Treasurys, as the issuance glut extends to corporate
debt. Investment-grade (IG) corporate issuance is running roughly 40% above last year
and is on pace for a record year. 1 While demand for Fixed Income remains healthy, the
sheer volume of new debt being brought to market is beginning to test investor capacity,
particularly at the long end of the Treasury curve and within certain sectors.

Hyperscalers Are Competing for Long-Duration Capital. A significant driver of
the surge in corporate issuance has been the hyperscalers. The largest technology
companies are financing an unprecedented wave of AI-related capital spending, leading
to nearly $280 billion of debt issuance year-to-date. 2 Many of these issuers carry high
credit ratings and have ramped up issuance of longer-maturity bonds. As a result, they
are increasingly competing with long-dated Treasurys for the same pool of institutional

capital, including insurance companies, pension funds, sovereign wealth funds and
duration-oriented Fixed Income investors—creating a crowding-out effect amongst high-
quality Fixed Income.
Fortunately, this is a case of pressure, not a credit crisis. Recent weakness in long-dated
Treasurys reflects a classic supply-and-demand imbalance and importantly is not a
solvency crisis for the U.S. government or high-quality hyperscalers. Investors are being

asked to absorb a growing volume of net new issuance from both the U.S. Treasury and
corporate America at the same time.
Debt Pressure and a Normalization, Not a Fiscal Reckoning. The rise in long-end
yields during August reflects this dynamic. The 30-year Treasury yield moved above
5.3%, reaching levels not seen since 2007. Fiscal concerns likely played a role, but we

### Page 5

5 of 8 September 14, 2026 – Capital Market Outlook RETURN TO FIRST PAGE
believe heavy supply, sticky inflation, reduced Fed demand and increased competition for
investor capital were equally important contributors.
And importantly, the market's adjustment mechanism is already working. Higher Treasury
yields and somewhat wider credit spreads are attracting buyers and helping clear the growing
supply of bonds. This is a natural normalization and is how markets restore equilibrium.

Recent Treasury auctions continue to attract demand consistent with historical norms, and
investor appetite for new IG issuance has remained robust. These are not characteristics
typically associated with a loss of confidence in U.S., or corporate, creditworthiness.
A New Yield Regime. For Fixed Income investors, the more important implication
is that a 4% to 5% 10-year Treasury yield may represent the new normal. In an
environment of very strong underlying economic growth and unprecedented volumes of

debt issuance from both the public and private sectors, higher yields may be required to
attract sufficient capital amid a more competitive landscape for yield.
We continue to view both nominal and real yields as attractive and believe investors
should remain focused on achieving strategic duration targets. While rate volatility may
persist, today's yield levels provide a potential opportunity to seek attractive income that
has been largely absent for much of the past decade.

The Bigger Picture: Growth Still Matters More Than Debt Levels. None of this dismisses
the long-term fiscal challenges facing the U.S. Debt levels are rising, interest costs
continue to grow, and mandatory spending increasingly consumes a larger share of the
federal budget. However, history shows that debt levels alone do not determine interest
rates. Between 2007 and 2020, U.S. debt rose dramatically even as the 10-year Treasury
yield fell from roughly 5% to below 1%. What ultimately matters is the interplay between

short-term interest rates, debt levels, economic growth, inflation and investor demand.
For instance, nominal GDP growth and the 10-year yield tend to be correlated, outside
of recessions—we believe recent rate increases partly reflect this strong macro
backdrop. Moreover, economic growth is running above long-end yields, supporting the
sustainability of rising government debt.

The U.S. continues to benefit from several structural advantages: the dollar remains
the world's reserve currency, Treasurys remain the foundation of the global financial

system, and U.S. capital markets are still the deepest and most liquid in the world. Those
advantages continue to support demand for U.S. assets and underpin sovereign credit
quality even as borrowing needs grow.
The bottom line, in our view, is that even though the recent rise in long-end yields catches
many headlines, there is no need to panic. Instead, the recent repricing reflects periodic
episodes of debt pressure rather than a fundamental loss of confidence in U.S. credit. With

borrowing demand from both Washington and corporate America likely to remain elevated,
bond prices may continue to serve as the market's release valve. The consequence is likely
a world of structurally higher long-term yields, not a sovereign debt crisis.

THE TICKER TAPE
What are the markets actually telling us?
Christopher Hyzy, Chief Investmen t Officer
When the markets speak loudly we should listen. There are often clues in the internal trends
within the markets that help us determine how far into an economic cycle we are or what drivers
of growth are most impactful. In addition, we can also find clues that help with determining

the micro picture. In other words, what industries are the most likely to benefit, from a
profitability standpoint is concerned, as the growth picture unfolds? Currently, the markets have
a megaphone, in our view. The year-to-date sharp outperformers have been consistent and
should continue to advance. These include commodities, including copper and oil, Emerging
Markets, Japan in the Developed Markets, Small-cap relative to Large-cap shares, Value versus

Growth, and more cyclically-oriented companies versus more defensive. This current consistent
outperformance informs us that we are still early- to mid-cycle in a supply-led expansion. We are
still early in the build out of the supply that is needed for the natural demand that is there. New
infrastructure is needed to handle the new economy that is progressing and quickly innovating.
This infrastructure needs commodities, all forms of energy, battery storage, minerals, metals,

recycled water, gases, chemicals, and all the compute and cloud capacity, including memory
and other components to handle the path toward mass tokenization. Moreover, the buildup of
government debt in the advanced economies as fiscal deficits widen is forcing large institutional
investors (including central banks and other government entities) to diversify their reserve
bases. This has increased the interest in gold holdings. Countries that have a vast amount of

natural resources, especially oil and gas, are in high demand by investors, which is obvious when
examining year-to-date outperformers. The bottom line is that the markets are telling us that we
are still early- to mid-cycle in this massive buildout, which will require a wide collection of hard
assets, Infrastructure (old and new), land, and within Equities, a more diversified approach to
exposure—particularly areas that are more cyclical- and Value-oriented. We expect this view to

extend itself well into the foreseeable future if not years.
In Fixed Income, it may seem more complex, but it is actually easier to understand what the
markets are telling us, in our opinion. The backup in
yields is “real yield” driven, which is more due
to higher nominal growth than to concerns of long-run inflation. This is evident in the Treasury
inflation protection market, break-evens in general and the ratio of Treasury yields relative to
other Go

vernment bond markets in the developed world. However, rising oil and gas prices and
inflation gauges that are still above the preferred level are usually the excuses for yields backing
up. Deficit concerns are a more plausible reason f
or yields rising but the main driver, in our view,
is still abo
ve average nominal growth. The yield curve and rates in general are still adjusting to
this new economic landscape that is very different than those of the most recent past.

Index Definitions
Securities indexes assume reinvestment of all distributions and interest payments. Indexes are unmanaged and do not take into account fees or expenses. It is not possible to
invest directly in an index. Indexes are all based in U.S. dollars.
S&P 500 Index is a stock market index tracking the stock performance of 500 leading companies listed on stock exchanges in the United States.

Bloomberg Commodity Index designed to provide liquid and diversified exposure to commodities via futures contracts. Is calculated on an excess return basis and reflects commodity
futures price movements.
MSCI Emerging Markets Index captures large and mid cap representation across 24 Emerging Markets countries. With 1,205 constituents, the index covers approximately 85% of the free
float adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI Japan Index is designed to measure the performance of the large and mid cap segments of the Japanese market.

MSCI Pacific ex Japan Index captures large and mid cap representation across 4 of 5 Developed Markets (DM) countries in the Pacific region (excluding Japan). With 93 constituents, the index
covers approximately 85% of the free float-adjusted market capitalization in each country.
MSCI All-Country World Index is a premier benchmark representing global equity performance, covering large and mid-cap stocks across 23 developed and 24 emerging markets.

MSCI World Index ex U.S. captures large and mid cap representation across 22 of 23 Developed Markets countries--excluding the United States..
MSCI USA Momentum Index is based on MSCI USA Index, its parent index, which captures large and mid-cap stocks of the U.S. market. It is designed to reflect the performance of an equity
momentum strategy by emphasizing stocks with high price momentum, while maintaining reasonably high trading liquidity, investment capacity and moderate index turnover.

U.S. Large-cap Growth/Russell 1000 Growth Index measures the performance of the large-cap growth segment of the U.S. equity universe. It includes those Russell 1000 companies with
relatively higher price-to-book ratios, higher I/B/E/S forecast medium term (2 year) growth and higher sales per share historical growth (5 years).
U.S. Small-cap/Russell 2000 Index measures the performance of the small-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe.
MSCI USA High Dividend Yield Index is designed to reflect the performance of equities in the MSCI USA Index (excluding REIT s) with higher dividend income and quality characteristics than

average dividend yields that are both sustainable and persistent.
U.S. Mid Cap/Russell Midcap Index measures the performance of the mid-cap segment of the U.S. Equity universe. The Russell Midcap Index is a subset of the Russell 1000® Index. It
includes approximately 800 of the smallest securities based on a combination of their market cap and current index membership.
MSCI USA Value Index captures large and mid-cap U.S. securities exhibiting overall value style characteristics.

MSCI USA Equal Weighted Index represents an alternative weighting scheme to its market cap weighted parent index, the MSCI USA Index. At each quarterly rebalance date, all index
constituents are weighted equally, effectively removing the influence of each constituent’s current price.
MSCI USA Minimum Volatility Index aims to reflect the performance characteristics of a minimum variance strategy applied to the large and mid-cap USA equity universe.

Investing involves risk, including the possible loss of principal. Past performance is no guarantee of future results.

Asset allocation, diversification and rebalancing do not ensure a profit or protect against loss in declining markets.
Investments have varying degrees of risk. Some of the risks involved with equity securities include the possibility that the value of the stocks may fluctuate in response to events specific to

the companies or markets, as well as economic, political or social events in the U.S. or abroad. Small cap and mid cap companies pose special risks, including possible illiquidity and greater price
volatility than funds consisting of larger, more established companies. Investing in fixed-income securities may involve certain risks, including the credit quality of individual issuers, possible
prepayments, market or economic developments and yields and share price fluctuations due to changes in interest rates. When interest rates go up, bond prices typically drop, and vice versa.

Investments in high-yield bonds (sometimes referred to as “junk bonds”) offer the potential for high current income and attractive total return, but involves certain risks. Changes in economic
conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect a junk bond issuer’s ability to make principal and interest payments. Income from investing in municipal bonds is generally exempt
from Federal and state taxes for residents of the issuing state. While the interest income is tax-exempt, any capital gains distributed are taxable to the investor. Income for some investors

may be subject to the Federal Alternative Minimum Tax (AMT). Treasury bills are less volatile than longer-term fixed income securities and are guaranteed as to timely payment of principal and
interest by the U.S. government. Bonds are subject to interest rate, inflation and credit risks. Investments in foreign securities (including ADRs) involve special risks, including foreign currency
risk and the possibility of substantial volatility due to adverse political, economic or other developments. These risks are magnified for investments made in emerging markets. Investments in

a certain industry or sector may pose additional risk due to lack of diversification and sector concentration. There are special risks associated with an investment in commodities, such as gold,
including market price fluctuations, regulatory changes, interest rate changes, credit risk, economic changes and the impact of adverse political or financial factors.
Alternative investments are speculative and involve a high degree of risk.
Alternative investments are intended for qualified investors only. Alternative Investments such as derivatives, hedge funds, private credit, private equity funds, and funds of funds can result

in higher return potential but also higher loss potential. Changes in economic conditions or other circumstances may adversely affect your investments. Before you invest in alternative
investments, you should consider your overall financial situation, how much money you have to invest, your need for liquidity, and your tolerance for risk.
Nonfinancial assets, such as closely held businesses, real estate, fine art, oil, gas and mineral properties, and timber, farm and ranch land, are complex in nature and involve risks including total

Document 236: CEE & CCA week ahead_ Hungarian and Czech growth, plus CCA rate decisions

Source PDF: all report pdf/CEE & CCA week ahead_ Hungarian and Czech growth, plus CCA rate decisions.pdf

### Page 1

Hungary and the Czech Republic will release third-quarter GDP
estimates next week, while Uzbekistan and Azerbaijan
announce their latest monetary policy decisions

Peter Virovacz, David Havrlant and Dmitry Dolgin
Hungary's growth picture remains mixed
Hungary: Flash GDP to provide ﬁrst look at
third-quarter growth
Friday brings the release of the preliminary Q3 GDP estimate. We
expect agriculture to have weighed heavily on growth in the third
quarter, but industry – mostly driven by the new export capacities –
should largely oﬀset this. Services will remain a major positive
contributor.
As this is only a ﬂash estimate, the data will provide few details.
Even so, annual growth should remain robust despite the quarterly
slowdown. We therefore maintain our full-year growth forecast of
1.9% and continue to expect growth to pick up in the ﬁnal quarter
of the year.

### Page 2

Czech Republic: Conﬁdence likely to
weaken as growth softens
Monday brings the release of October conﬁdence indicators, and on
Friday we'll get the preliminary 3Q26 GDP estimate. We expect
economic performance to have weakened further on an annual
basis in 3Q26, but quarter-on-quarter growth should have picked
up thanks to a pre-stocking eﬀect triggered by the Hormuz conﬂict.
Large numbers of people submitted new orders when it became
clear that prices would rise and products would become more

scarce.
That said, we assume this positive eﬀect will fade at the year's turn.
Both business and consumer conﬁdence likely deteriorated in
October, reﬂecting rising prices, the bond-market selloﬀ, the
propensity for tighter monetary policy, and the crisis in France.
CCA: Uzbekistan and Azerbaijan central
banks expected to stay on hold
Wednesday brings the Central Bank of Uzbekistan's latest interest
rate decision. We expect the CBRU to keep the policy rate

unchanged at 14.00%, although a 50bp cut remains a close
alternative. A hold appears appropriate given an increasingly pro-
inﬂationary and hawkish global backdrop, some recent weakening
in the soum and still-strong domestic demand. At the same time,
the case for easing continues to build, as core inﬂation has recently
stabilised at 5.5% YoY, not far above the 5% target, real interest
rates remain high, and domestic bank funding continues to
materially outperform lending growth, pointing to restrictive

monetary conditions. We therefore see a hold as a tactical pause
before gradual easing in the medium term.
The Central Bank of Azerbaijan announces its latest interest rate
decision on Thursday. We expect the CBA to keep the reﬁnancing
rate unchanged at 6.50%, as inﬂation remains within the 4±2%
target range. Headline CPI eased to 5.5% YoY in September, arguing
against tightening. However, CPI remains relatively close to the
upper end of the target range, while domestic producer-price

Document 253: De-reservification, Not De-dollarization (2026-09-15)

Source PDF: all report pdf/De-reservification, Not De-dollarization (2026-09-15).pdf
Report source: CFR (Follow the Money)
Date: 2026-09-15
Pages: 17
Status: ok

### Page 1

1
EXPERT TAKE FOLLOW THE MONEY
De-reservification, Not De-dollarization
The world isn’t moving away from the dollar. It is shifting its dollars from traditional
reserves to state banks, pension funds, and other quasi-sovereign investors.
(Bernardo Montoya/Courtesy Reuters)
PUBLISHED
September 15, 2026 8:37 a.m.
Articles Follow the Money Share
Log In
Regions Topics Research Experts Events The Future of American Strategy

### Page 2

2
Brad W. Setser CFR EXPERT
Whitney Shepardson Senior Fellow
This is a joint blog with Alex Etra, Senior Macro
Strategist and head of Flow of Funds at Vanda
Research.
The IMF’s data on the currency composition of
foreign exchange (FX) reserves is scrutinized carefully
for any signs that the world is shifting way from the
dollar.
Yet it doesn’t really matter that much if the dollar’s
share of reserves has shifted from 56.5 percent to 57
percent. The ink-to-impact ratio of the quarterly

reporting on the latest COFER data is all off.
Neither the stock of global reserves nor the stock of
dollar reserves has changed much in the last ten years.
The action is elsewhere.

### Page 3

3
China’s state banks (per the Bank of International
Settlements) have almost as many foreign assets as the
central bank (PBOC).
Japan’s Government Pension Investment Fund
(GPIF) has almost as many foreign assets ($986
billion) as the government has FX reserves ($1.1
trillion at the end of August). Japan’s FX reserves are
on the books of the Ministry of Finance and the
GPIF is overseen by the Ministry of Health, Welfare
and Labor—so these are almost all assets of the

Government of Japan or the broader public sector,
not its central bank.
Korea’s National Pension Service has more foreign
assets than the Bank of Korea has FX reserves.
A different dynamic is in play in Taiwan, as the
government doesn’t run a big retirement fund. But in

### Page 4

4
a world where the hedge ratio of the life insurers has
been (and still is) used as a policy tool, the shift in
Taiwan’s foreign asset accumulation away from the
Central Bank of China (CBC) and over to
institutions whose activities in the foreign currency
market it directly influences broadly fits into the same
theme. So too does the PBOC’s use of the
“macroprudential adjustment factor” effectively adjust
banks’ net foreign assets in much the same way that

CBC regulations adjust lifer hedge ratios.
In the last year, the lifers’ hedge ratios—and their
direct hedges with the central bank—have been one
of the key tools the CBC has used to manage the
Taiwan dollar (and guard the lifers’ solvency).
Asia’s big surplus economies are in a sense just
catching up with the world’s oil exporters. Norway
put its oil surplus into a sovereign wealth fund from
the start. Kuwait, Qatar, and Abu Dhabi (the most
oil-rich emirate) all also have large sovereign funds.

And the world’s biggest oil exporter has joined them:
The Public Investment Fund has almost as many
foreign assets as the Saudi Central Bank (SAMA).*
What’s more, the available evidence suggests that the
dollar share of these pools is in line with or higher
than the dollar share of global FX reserves (it depends
on the institution and jurisdiction).

### Page 5

5
In other words, by looking at the (easily available)
data on the world’s static holdings of formal FX
reserve assets, scholars and analysts miss most of the
growth in the world’s sovereign and quasi-sovereign
foreign assets.
Consider China.
No serious analyst now disputes that China’s state
banks—including the policy banks (the China
Development Bank, China Exim)—hold several
trillion in foreign assets.
China reports $3.3 trillion in gross foreign assets to

the BIS (the net position, counting foreign bank
claims on the entire Chinese economy not just the
banks, is $2.5 trillion). That maps to the BOP data,
which shows almost $4 trillion in gross outflows
through the banking system (technically, the sum of
gross outflows in “other” plus the $500 billion in
foreign currency bonds held by the state commercial
banks)

### Page 6

6
Since 2010 (and even more so after 2014) the net
outflow through the state banks has exceeded reserve
accumulation.
The broad contours of this story are confirmed by the
balance sheet data reported by state commercial
banks in their 2025 annual reports, which showed

### Page 7

7
that the top five banks held a combined $2.5 trillion
in foreign currency assets (mostly held abroad).
The Chinese haven’t disclosed the foreign assets of
the two policy banks (with at this stage the complicity
of the IMF, which has neither analyzed the role of
SAFE policy bank financing, nor highlighted the
glaring gap in China’s own reporting). But the work
of AidData points to nearly $1 trillion in foreign
assets, with a hefty dollar share.
The available data sources all suggest that the bulk of

banks—puts the dollar share of their offshore foreign
currency assets at around 70 percent. That is well
above the last disclosed dollar share of China’s formal
reserves (55 percent in 2019). The banks’ annual

### Page 8

8
reports suggest that about 70 percent of the banks’
assets are in dollars and Hong Kong dollars (55
percent USD/15 percent HKD)—a bit below the
dollar and Hong Kong dollar share of their liabilities
(swaps out of the HKD fund a decent share of the
banks euro assets).
Put simply, SAFE’s static dollar holdings aren’t the
important story.
The real story is the rapid growth of the state banks
dollar holdings.
Japan holds a high (though undisclosed) dollar share

in its FX reserves, and those reserves are primarily
invested in Treasuries—so Japan’s MoF is now clearly
the largest contributor to the U.S. data on foreign
official holdings of Treasuries. (SAFE has shifted its
funds out of U.S. custodians, and thus increasingly
appears in the data as a “private” holder in a European
custodial center)
Japan’s formal reserves haven’t changed much in the
last ten years; periodic sales to limit the yen’s weakness
have offset most of the MoF’s accumulated interest

income. Foreign currency reserves were $1.2 trillion
in 2014 and $1.16 trillion at the end of Q1. They
dipped to about $1 trillion at the end of August after
sales of close to $100 billion at the end of July/start of
August.

### Page 9

9
What has grown is the foreign portfolio of the GPIF.
it was around $400 billion in 2014, ~$1 trillion in Q1
of 2026.
GPIF’s foreign bonds aren’t held exclusively in dollars,
but roughly 52 percent of its foreign bonds (26
percent of all bonds) are USD-denominated.
But the GPIF, like most international investors, holds
a large share of its equity portfolio in the U.S. (~$165
billion, 65 percent of the foreign equity portfolio).
Korea is a more extreme version of Japan.

The Bank of Korea’s FX reserves have been roughly
$375-450 billion over the last ten years or so. Around
two-thirds of those are now in dollars (as of the end of
2025).

### Page 10

10
From 2012 to 2025, the foreign assets at the NPS
have grown from ~$80 billion (around 21 percent of
total assets) to over $660 billion (55 percent of total
assets)—and something like 70 percent of those are in
dollars. 76 percent of the fund’s equities are in dollars,
and 66 percent of its debt. And while a lower share of
its “alternative” portfolio is in North America, most
alternative mandates are awarded in dollars.

### Page 11

11
In other words, the majority of the dollar assets held
by Korea’s government (the NPS reports to the
Ministry of Health and Welfare, and the Minister of
Health chairs its asset allocation committee) aren’t
held by the central bank and aren’t in the IMF’s
COFER data set.
Taiwan is an unusual case, as the life insurers aren’t
technically sovereign investors. But with their
regulator now explicitly encouraging the them to hold
an unhedged foreign currency book and hence they

now rely on the state for their solvency, they have a bit
of an official character (a significant appreciation of
the TWD would blow through their volatility reserves
and leave them with fewer assets than liabilities, as
their foreign assets are largely held against TWD
policies). The CBC certainly now uses the lifers desire
to unwind existing hedges to help manage the Taiwan

### Page 12

12
dollar. And they have an exceptionally high dollar
share in their foreign assets—over 95 percent.
The Saudi Public Investment Fund (PIF) is another
example.
It keeps roughly $200 billion of its $250 billion in
foreign investments in dollars—well above the U.S.
share of a standard global stock index. Almost all of
the PIF’s $30 billion in external debt is also in dollars.
And the PIF now has only slightly fewer foreign
assets than the Saudi Central Bank has reserves.**

The broad story is thus pretty clear: the growth in the
world’s sovereign and quasi-sovereign assets is not
coming through an increase in FX reserve holdings
managed by the world’s central banks.

### Page 13

13
And, in the critical case of China, the dollar share of
China’s quasi-sovereign foreign assets is likely higher
than the dollar share of their formal FX reserves.
Adam Tooze has noted that inflows into the U.S. are
now coming from investors looking for a profit, not
investors looking for safety. What he calls the “profit
dollar” manifests itself in a number of different ways.
More inflows into corporate bonds and equities for
one. Less official demand for Treasuries for another.

More opacity too.
Those same trends are at play among sovereign and
quasi-sovereign investors.
So don’t obsess about the dollar’s chare in formal FX
reserves. Do recognize that the dollar’s “reserve
currency role” isn’t the source of any significant new
inflows into the dollar.

### Page 14

14
The dollar’s dominant role in the international
monetary system depends on much more than the
size and composition of central bank FX reserves. It is
as much, perhaps more so (given that FX reserve
managers are themselves ultimately liability matchers)
a function of the portfolio choice of a set of
private/semi-private/quasi-sovereign investors that
are much more difficult to observe. Reserve currency
status is not only about FX reserves in a strict sense.

That’s been true for some time. Most of the current
flow into U.S. from the “official” sector is coming
from investors who are not classic reserve managers.
Those flows remain heavily tilted toward the dollar, at
least for now.***
And any real de-dollarization would likely occur first
among these investors.
Put differently, the dollar’s global role is increasingly
as a source of returns, not a source of safety. The
foreign bid, private and public, is for risk, not for

Treasuries. That doesn’t help Scott Bessent much
right now, but it has helped keep valuations in the
stock market extended. And the global debate on the
dollar’s role lags the evolution in the dollar’s role, and
the risks associated with that new role.
It is currently fashionable in some circles to point to
the diminution of the “convenience yield” on

### Page 15

15
Treasuries even while the U.S. dollar continues to
enjoy such a privilege. But the shift in the global
investor base, and their apparent portfolio preference
for U.S. risk assets in lieu of Treasuries, may help
explain the divergence between the convenience yield
on UST and the convenience yield on USD.
And of course, if the negative convenience yield
implies that Treasuries are trading at a historical
discount, the persistence of the latter—and its

concentration in risk assets—suggests that the “profit
dollar” itself is likely trading at a historical premium.
That apparent premium on U.S. risk assets deserves as
much (or more) scrutiny as the currency composition
of central bank FX reserves.
* The PIF and Saudi Aramco also now have a certain
amount of debt.
** Reserves (now mostly in Treasuries, so should
appear in the TIC data, unlike at some times in the
past.

### Page 16

16
This work represents the views solely of the author(s). The
Council on Foreign Relations is an independent,
nonpartisan membership organization, think tank, and
publisher, and takes no institutional positions on matters
of policy.

*** Japan is the main exception; the GPIF’s dollar
share is below the likely dollar share of Japan’s MoF.
